Need implement LP step robustly. Let's define:

```
def lp_step(f, M, max_time_lp=20):
    n=len(f)
    # Build A dense: shape (2n-1,n)
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i, val in enumerate(f):
        if val != 0:
            A[i:i+n, i] = val
    # maybe scale b/M? A g <= M. We can scale rows by M to reduce? linprog handles.
    c = -np.ones(n)
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M), bounds=(0, None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': max_time_lp, 'presolve': True})
    if res.success: return res.x
    # If fail, maybe return f
```

Memory: For n=1024, A dense 2047x1024 ~2M floats=16MB. Building each iteration maybe 0.01? LP time maybe 0.1-1s. Good. For n=2048, 4095x2048~8.4M=67MB, maybe okay but LP seconds. We can use sparse to reduce memory? Total nonzeros n^2, same; dense maybe okay. Sparse build using `scipy.sparse.lil_matrix` then csr; 1M nonzeros memory ~16MB (data+indices+indptr ~8+4+4=16) plus overhead. Dense simpler. For K=1024 dense 16MB, constraints 2k; fine. Let's implement fast convolution max and sum:

```
def fft_convolve_max(a):
    n=np.size(a)
    L=1
    target=2*n-1
    while L<target: L*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:target]
    # due to numerical small imaginary? irfft real. set negative small to 0? For max, max(b) okay. Could round negative tiny.
    return float(np.max(b)), float(np.sum(a))
```

But for each evaluation, sum can be precomputed; in local search sum changes. We can compute score from a quickly.

```
def make_exponential(n, beta, decreasing=False):
    if n<=1: return np.ones(n)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    if decreasing: x=1-x
    return np.exp(beta*x)
```

Let's design SOTA wrapper:

```
def sota_improve(f, max_iters, time_limit_start, verbose=True):
    n=len(f)
    S0=math.sqrt(2*n)
    f = f / sum(f) * S0
    cur = normalized_score(f)  # max conv after normalization, = score
    best=f.copy(); best_score=cur
    start=time.monotonic()
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() - start > time_limit or (time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 300): break
        M=normalized_score_with_conv(f) # also conv? We can compute max; maybe need not conv.
        g = lp_step(f, M, ...)
        if g is None: break
        gsum = sum(g)
        if gsum <= 1e-12: break
        g = g / gsum * S0
        # line search
        improved=False
        for t in (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.333, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01):
            h=(1-t)*f+t*g
            hscore=normalized_score(h)
            if hs < cur - 1e-10:
                f=h; cur=hs; improved=True; break
        if improved:
            if cur < best_score: best=f.copy(); best_score=cur
        else:
            # try smaller random t or perturb g
            # maybe perturb g with noise
            for t in np.linspace(0,1,20): ...
            if not improved:
                # add random noise to f and perhaps break to different start
                f = clip(f + noise)
                normalize
        # stagnation: if cur not improved for many, break or perturb
    return best, best_score
```

```
def objective_norm(a):
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    a=a/s*math.sqrt(2*len(a))
    conv=fast_conv(a)
    return float(conv.max())
```

```
def upsample(a, n):
    if len(a)==n: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.r_[0,1]; x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
```

```
       g /= len(active_sample)
```

Random block perturbations:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, sigma_start, temperature):
    n=len(a)
    width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//512), max(2,n//32)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
    b=np.clip(a.copy(),0,None)
    b[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, size=width))
    # occasionally global small noise
    if rng.random()<0.2: b *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.01,n))? clip
    return normalize(b)
```

Fast convolution:
```
def fast_conv_full(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    target=2*n-1
    if n <= 1024: # maybe direct for accuracy? O(1M) fine
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    L=1
    while L < target: L <<= 1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:target]
    # clean tiny negatives
    out[np.abs(out) < 1e-12] = 0.0
    return out
```
But calling fft many times; direct for n=512 maybe fine. Use direct for n<=512? 512^2=262k, fast; for 1024=1M, still fast; direct might be accurate and simpler. But in LP loops, objective called many times with K=1024; 1M per eval * thousands = too slow. Use FFT for all n > 256. For n=128, direct.

```
def normalized_score_with_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf, np.array([])
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300: return inf, a
    scale=math.sqrt(2.0*n)/s
    a=a*scale
    conv=fast_conv_full(a)
    m=float(np.max(conv)) if conv.size else 0.0
    return m, a, conv  # maybe return conv for active
```
To avoid returning too much, separate:
```
def objective_score(a):
    conv = normalized_conv(a)
    return float(np.max(conv))
```
But local search needs conv. We'll implement:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum=None):
    n=a.size
    if target_sum is None: target_sum=math.sqrt(2*n)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return a*0
    return a * (target_sum/s)
```
`normalized_conv(a)` returns conv of normalized a. `score_from_conv(conv) = max`.

Candidate generation and upsample:
```
def upsample_linear(a, n):
    m=len(a)
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if m==1: return np.full(n, a[0])
    xold=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    xnew=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(xnew, xold, a)
```
`upsample_repeat(a,n)`: tile `np.tile(a, n//m)[:n]` maybe plus remainder. If n not multiple, use `np.resize`.

LP matrix builder:
```
def build_conv_matrix(f):
    n=f.size
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    # Use for i,val in enumerate(f): A[i:i+n, i] = val
    for i, val in enumerate(f):
        if val:
            A[i:i+n, i] = val
    return A
```
For n=1024 loop 1024, each slice assignment length up to 1024 -> 1M. If f many zeros? no.

Potential performance improvement: Build A via `np.empty` and use `fill_diagonal`? The matrix has columns as f_i in a band. Could use `scipy.sparse` to reduce memory and maybe faster? Highs with sparse may be slower but memory lower. Dense okay.

LP step:
```
def lp_direction(f, M, n_vars=None, time_limit=15.0):
    n=len(f)
    if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    A=build_conv_matrix(f)
    c=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M)
    try:
        res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs',
                    options={'time_limit': time_limit, 'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
        return None
    if res.success:
        x=np.asarray(res.x, float)
        if np.all(np.isfinite(x)) and x.sum()>0:
            return np.clip(x,0,None)
    # If not success but maybe have x?
    if hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
       ...
    return None
```
But building A for each iteration; we could also use a wrapper to avoid memory if n>1500. Set max_lp_n = 1500 maybe. For K=2048, A dense 67MB; okay but might slow. We'll use max 1536 or 2048 based on memory. 1 CPU. Let's set max_lp_n=1536 to be safe. K=2048 maybe optional if time and memory; 67MB okay, but HiGHS on 8M constraints? Actually constraints 4095, vars 2048, nonzeros 4M? Dense 8M. Might be ~1s. Could try. We'll make adaptive: for compressed improvement, use K=1024, maybe then K=2048 if time and no OOM.

SOTA wrapper:
```
def sota_search(f, time_budget, max_iters=200, min_improve=1e-10, verbose=True):
    f=normalize(f)
    cur=score(f)
    best=f.copy(); best_score=cur
    start=time.monotonic()
    stag=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic()-start > time_budget: break
        # get conv maybe current
        conv=normalized_conv(f)
        m=float(conv.max())
        # if m < best_score best
        g=lp_direction(f,m, time_limit=min(20, ...))
        if g is None: break
        g=normalize(g)
        # line search
        improved=False
        # use current f/g sums same; t choices dynamically
        t_candidates = [0.5,0.25,0.1,0.75,1.0,0.05,0.2,0.333,0.02]
        # maybe use ternary line search for first few? Simpler.
        for t in t_candidates:
             h=(1-t)*f+t*g
             sc=score(h)
             if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                  best=h.copy(); best_score=sc
             if sc < cur - 1e-11:
                  f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
        if improved:
             stag=0
        else:
             stag += 1
             # try random t and perturbation
             for t in np.linspace(0,1,10):
                 h=(1-t)*f+t*g; sc=score(h)
                 if sc<cur-1e-11: f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
             if not improved:
                 # small perturbation of f to escape
                 rng local? Use global rng? We can pass seed.
                 f = normalize(f + np.random.default_rng(...).normal(0,0.01*f.std(), n)? )
                 cur=score(f)
                 if cur < best_score: best=f...
                 if stag > 20: break
    return best,best_score
```
Need RNG state to be reproducible. Use `np.random.default_rng(seed + phase)` in function. Since no closures.
But using random perturbations inside SOTA may break theoretical descent; okay.

```
def optimize_piecewise_poly(n, time_budget, seed):

       # quick eval
```

`basis_eval` returns score for P coefficients. It will allocate exp each call; K=256, okay.
```
def basis_eval(p, K, powers):
    P = p[0]
    for i in range(1,len(p)): P = P + p[i]*powers[i-1]
    P = P - P.min()
    f = np.exp(P)
    s=f.sum()
    if s<=0: return 1e100
    f = f / s * math.sqrt(2*K)
    conv=fast_conv_full(f)
    return float(np.max(conv))
```
`powers` is list of arrays. No closure.

Nelder-Mead with args: `minimize(basis_eval, x0, args=(K,powers), method='Nelder-Mead', options=...)`.

Downsample function:
```
def downsample(a, m):
    n=len(a)
    if n<=m: return upsample? if n<m, return np.resize(a,m)
    # average blocks of size n/m
    indices=np.linspace(0,n,m+1).astype(int)
    blocks=[a[indices[i]:indices[i+1]] for i...]; return np.array([b.mean() if b.size else 0])
```

Now, local search final. Let's design more robust:

`polish_sequence(a, budget_s, rng, print_every=...)`:
- Normalize a to S0.
- `best=a; best_score=score(a)`.
- Maintain a current `x=best`.
- Loop until time:
   1. Subgradient step: compute conv; active top coefficients; gradient; try several steps. If improved, accept and reset `stagnation`. Else count.
   2. Random block perturbation: choose sigma maybe from `0.1` down to `0.01` based on stagnation. Use multiple perturbations per iteration (e.g., 5). Accept best improvement.
   3. If no improvement for many, try larger perturbation / random restart around best (e.g., multiply by exp(N(0,0.05))) or revert to best.
- Since objective quasi-convex? Keep best.

Need efficient conv for subgradient. We'll compute conv via `normalized_conv` which normalizes; but if x normalized, no rescale? Use `fast_conv(x)` after ensuring normalized. For score, max.

Subgradient implementation vectorized for a set of top coefficients:
```
def add_coeff_gradient(g, a, k):
    n=len(a)
    if k < n:
        # term1: j=0..k
        g[:k+1] += a[k::-1]
        # term2: j=k..n-1
        g[k:] += a[:n-k]
    else:
        lo = k - n + 1
        # term1: j=lo..n-1, a[k-j] indices n-1 down to k-lo+1? Let's derive.
        # For j=lo, k-j = k-(k-n+1)=n-1. For j=n-1, index = k-(n-1)=k-n+1=lo.
        # So a[n-1:lo-1:-1] length n-lo.
        g[lo:] += a[n-1:lo-1:-1] if lo>0 else a[n-1::-1]
```
Check for k=n-1 (which is <n? k=n-1 branch): g[:n] += a[n-1::-1]; g[n-1:] += a[:1]. correct. For k=n (else, lo=1): g[1:] += a[n-1:0:-1] (length n-1). Variable j=0 not in coefficient n? c_n = sum i=n? For n length, c_n terms i=1..n-1? Yes j=0 no. Good.
For k=2n-2, lo=n-1: g[n-1:] += a[n-1:n-2:-1] = a[n-1]. correct.

We can average gradient over selected active coefficients:
```
g=np.zeros(n)
for k in active_list: add_coeff_gradient(g,a,k)
g/=len(active_list)
gp = g - np.mean(g)
```

However, gradient projection to zero sum: `gp = g - mean(g)`. After update, renormalize. Because of nonnegativity, clipping breaks sum; normalize back.

Random block perturbations: We can use multiplicative exp with sigma relative to current. For x normalized sum S0, local typical value S0/n. Multiplicative sigma 0.1 changes few entries. But to change shape, block width maybe n/16 to n/4. We can also apply additive Gaussian with std 0.01*S0/√n? Multiplicative easier.

```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    b=a.copy()
    width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//64), max(2,n//8)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
    b[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, size=width)))
    if rng.random()<0.2:
       b *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma*0.1)))
    b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    return normalize_sequence(b)
```
Also allow uniform noise over all entries with small sigma:
```
if rng.random()<0.1: b = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma*0.2, n)); clip normalize
```

Optimize basis:
```
def optimize_basis(K, time_budget, rng):
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    x=np.linspace(0,1,K)
    powers=[x**i for i in range(6)]
    best_p=None; best_score=inf
    # starts: analytic + random
    starts=[np.array([0.0,1.25,0.0,0.0,0.0,0.0]), ...]
    for i in range(30):
       p=np.zeros(6); p[0]=0; p[1:]=rng.uniform(-2,2,5); starts.append(p)
    for p0 in starts:
       if time.monotonic()-start_time > time_budget: break
       sc=basis_eval(p0,K,powers)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_p=p0.copy()
       # Nelder-Mead with small budget based on remaining
       rem=time_budget - (time.monotonic()-start_time)
       if rem < 2: continue
       try:
          res=minimize(basis_eval, p0, args=(K,powers), method='Nelder-Mead',
                       options={'maxiter':200, 'xatol':1e-5, 'fatol':1e-7, 'adaptive':True})
          if res.success or (res.x is not None):
             sc=float(basis_eval(res.x,K,powers))
             if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_p=res.x.copy()
       except Exception:
          pass
    # final random perturbation around best
    if best_p is not None and time...:
       for _ in range(100): p=best_p+rng.normal(0,0.1,6); ...
    f=None
    if best_p is not None: f=basis_values(best_p,K,powers)
    else: f=None
    return f,best_score
```
Need `basis_values` top-level:
```
def basis_values(p,K,powers=None):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,K) if powers None ... but Nelder calls need powers. We'll pass powers.
    P=p[0] + ...
    P-=P.min()
    f=np.exp(P)
    return normalize_sequence(f)
```

If variable j = first index i, derivative includes a_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n. If variable j = second index (k-i=j => i=k-j), derivative includes a_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n. These are the same condition! For k=0, j=1: k-j=-1 invalid, no contribution. My term2 condition should be `0 <= k-j < n`, not `0 <= j-k < n`. I mistakenly used j-k. Since c_k not symmetric in indices? I previously added term for j-k because I thought autocorrelation. Correct derivative for convolution with same sequence: ∂/∂a_j ∑_i a_i a_{k-i} = δ_{i=j} a_{k-j} + a_i δ_{k-i=j} = a_{k-j} if k-j valid + a_i if i=k-j valid. Because second index j would require first index k-j, which is invalid. So condition is just k-j valid. Good.

General vectorized way: `idx = np.arange(lo, hi+1); g[idx] += 2*a[k-idx]`. This allocates length up to n; for top few active, okay. Simpler and less bug.
```
def add_coeff_gradient(g,a,k):
    n=a.size
    lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
    if lo>hi: return
    idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
    g[idx] += 2.0 * a[k-idx]
```

Let's implement `fast_conv_full` efficiently:
```
def fast_conv_full(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 128:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    target = 2*n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < target: L <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)
    out = out[:target]
    # numerical cleanup: set small negative to zero
    # Avoid allocating mask too large? fine.
    out[out < 0] = 0.0  # but if true negative? nonnegative, okay; can discard real small negative. If a nonnegative, true conv nonneg, so negative due to rounding.
    return out
```
`out[out<0]=0` will modify; if large negative due to bug, hides. Okay.

For n=16384, L=32768, out length 32768; good.

`normalize_sequence`:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target=None):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()? copy maybe expensive. We can avoid copy if scaling.
    if arr.size==0: return arr
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros_like(arr)
    if target is None: target = math.sqrt(2.0*arr.size)
    return arr * (target / s)
```
It returns new array. For line search many times, allocation okay.

`objective_score`:
```
def objective_score(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a,float)
    if arr.size==0: return INF
    n=arr.size
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): return INF
    arr = np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<0.01: return INF
    # We can compute score directly without normalizing to sqrt(2n) via formula to avoid scale: max conv / s^2 *2n. But conv scales with values. Normalize to target:
    arr = arr * (math.sqrt(2*n)/s)
    conv = fast_conv_full(arr)
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    # due to FFT, m may slightly negative? no.
    if not math.isfinite(m): return INF
    return m
```
Since after normalization sum target, score = m exactly. But if we clip to 1000 before normalize? The evaluator clips after converting; values won't exceed 1000 because sum target small; but safe. The exact score from normalized is same as evaluator up to clipping (no cap hit). Good.

`normalized_conv`:
```
def normalized_conv(a): return fast_conv_full(normalize_sequence(a))
```

`exact_score` uses evaluate_sequence, but if evaluate_sequence not defined? It should be. If not, fallback objective? In propose, assume. We'll wrap.

`upsample_linear`:
```
def upsample_linear(a,n):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); m=a.size
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if m<=1: return np.full(n, a[0] if m else 0.0)
    xold=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    xnew=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    b=np.interp(xnew, xold, a)
    return normalize_sequence(b)
```
Normalizing changes endpoints? okay.

`upsample_repeat`:
```
def upsample_repeat(a,n):
    m=a.size
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    reps = max(1, int(np.ceil(n/m)))
    b = np.tile(a, reps)[:n]
    return normalize_sequence(b)
```

`make_exp`:
```
def make_exp(n,beta,reverse=False):
    if n<=1: return np.ones(n)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    if reverse: x=1-x
    f=np.exp(beta*x)
    return normalize_sequence(f)
```

`optimize_basis`:
Need `basis_eval` robust for Nelder-Mead. Since Nelder-Mead may pass list; convert.
```
def basis_eval(p,K,powers):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    if p.size != len(powers): return INF
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)): return INF
    P = np.zeros(K, float)
    for i, pow in enumerate(powers):
        P += p[i]*pow
    P -= np.min(P)
    f = np.exp(P)
    s=f.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0: return INF
    f = f * (math.sqrt(2.0*K)/s)
    conv=fast_conv_full(f)
    m=float(np.max(conv))
    return m if math.isfinite(m) else INF
```
`basis_values` similar but returns f.

`optimize_basis` details:
```
def optimize_basis(K,budget_s,rng):
    start=time.monotonic()
    x=np.linspace(0,1,K)
    deg=5
    powers=[x**i for i in range(deg+1)]
    best_p=None; best_score=INF
    starts=[]
    for b in [1.25,1.0,1.5,2.0,-1.25]:
       p=np.zeros(deg+1); p[1]=b; starts.append(p)
    for _ in range(50):
       p=np.zeros(deg+1); p[0]=0; p[1:]=rng.uniform(-2,2,deg); starts.append(p)
    for idx,p0 in enumerate(starts):
       rem=budget_s - (time.monotonic()-start)
       if rem < 1: break
       sc=basis_eval(p0,K,powers)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_p=p0.copy()
       # Only run Nelder-Mead if score promising or index small? To save time, run for first 10 plus each time if sc<best_score.
       if HAVE_SCIPY and rem > 5:
          options={'maxiter':200,'xatol':1e-5,'fatol':1e-7,'adaptive':True}
          try:
             res=minimize(basis_eval, p0, args=(K,powers), method='Nelder-Mead', options=options)
             if hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
                sc2=basis_eval(res.x,K,powers)
                if sc2<best_score:
                   best_score=sc2; best_p=np.asarray(res.x,float).copy()
          except Exception:
             pass
       # occasional refine with random jitter around best if best exists
    if best_p is not None:
       # random local polish in parameter space
       for _ in range(200):
          if time.monotonic()-start > budget_s-0.1: break
          p=best_p + rng.normal(0,0.05,deg+1); p[0]=0
          sc=basis_eval(p,K,powers)
          if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_p=p.copy()
    if best_p is None:
       f=None
    else:
       f=basis_values(best_p,K,powers)
    return f,best_score
```
But Nelder-Mead for 30 starts * 200 iterations each could be 6000 evals; K=128 direct/FFT, okay maybe 10s? Each eval maybe 0.001s ->6s plus overhead. But with 2400s, fine. If time budget 180s, no issue. Could be slow due to scipy minimize Python calls; still okay. We can reduce starts to 12. Let's set 12 random + 6 analytic =18, maxiter 150. Good.

`basis_values(p,K,powers=None)`:
```
def basis_values(p,K,powers=None):
    if powers is None:
       x=np.linspace(0,1,K); powers=[x**i for i in range(len(p))]
    P=np.zeros(K,float)
    for i,pow in enumerate(powers): P += p[i]*pow
    P-=np.min(P)
    f=np.exp(P)
    return normalize_sequence(f)
```

```
def build_conv_matrix(f):
    n=f.size
    A=np.empty((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    A.fill(0.0)
    for i,val in enumerate(f):
       if val != 0.0:
          A[i:i+n, i] = val
    return A
```

`lowpass_sequence` maybe:
```
def lowpass_sequence(a, win_frac=0.1):
    n=a.size; win=max(1,int(n*win_frac))
    if win==1: return a
    k=np.ones(win)/win
    if win <= 32:
       return np.convolve(a,k,mode='same')
    L=1; target=2*n-1; while L<target: L*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L); fk=np.fft.rfft(k,L); out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fk,L)[:n]
    return out
```
But `mode='same'` for odd/even length? Okay. Use only if time not critical.

SOTA loop with time:
```
def sota_search(f,budget_s,rng,verbose=True):
    f=normalize_sequence(np.clip(np.asarray(f,float),0,None))
    if f.size <2: return f, objective_score(f)
    cur=objective_score(f.copy())
    best=f.copy(); best_score=cur
    start=time.monotonic()
    no_improve=0
    max_no_improve=20
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s and no_improve<max_no_improve:
       rem=budget_s - (time.monotonic()-start)
       if rem < 1: break
       n=f.size
       if n > 1500 and HAVE_LP? maybe skip if no scipy.
       conv=normalized_conv(f); m=float(np.max(conv))
       if m < best_score - 1e-12: best=f.copy(); best_score=m
       M = m*(1+1e-8)+1e-10
       g = lp_direction(f, M, min(20, rem/10))
       if g is None:
          # try perturb and break?
          break
       g = normalize_sequence(g)
       directions = [g]
       gm = 0.5*(g + f)
       directions.append(normalize_sequence(gm))
       if n <= 1024:
          gl = lowpass_sequence(g, 0.1)
          if any finite: directions.append(normalize_sequence(gl))
       improved=False
       t_list = (0.5,0.25,0.1,0.75,1.0,0.05,0.2,0.333,0.02,0.01)
       for d in directions:
          if not np.all(np.isfinite(d)): continue
          for t in t_list:
             h=(1-t)*f + t*d
             h=np.clip(h,0,None)
             sc=objective_score(h)
             if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                best=h.copy(); best_score=sc
             if sc < cur - 1e-10:
                f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
          if improved: break
       if improved:
          no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          # maybe try many random t all d
          for d in directions:
             ts=np.linspace(0,1,9)
             for t in ts:
                h=...; sc=...
                if sc<cur-1e-10: f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
             if improved: break
          if not improved:
             # reset f to best to avoid drift, add small random perturbation
             f = best.copy()
             cur = objective_score(f)
             if no_improve > max_no_improve: break
       it+=1
    return best,best_score
```
But if f reset to best, next LP from best; good.

Now, lowpass function:
```
def lowpass_sequence(a, frac=0.1):
   n=a.size
   win=int(max(1, round(n*frac)))
   if win<=1: return a.copy()
   if win %2 ==0: win +=1
   if win>=n: win=n
   k=np.ones(win,float)/win
   if win <= 64:
      out=np.convolve(a,k,mode='same')
   else:
      L=1
      while L < max(2*n, win): L <<=1
      out=np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,L)*np.fft.rfft(k,L), L)[:n]
   return np.clip(out,0,None)
```
`np.convolve(a,k,'same')` returns length n. Good. But for n=1024, win=102, direct O(100k) fine.

Now, local polish sequence. Let's implement:
```
def polish_sequence(a,budget_s,rng,verbose=False):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,None)
    if a.size==0: return a,INF
    start=time.monotonic()
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    cur=objective_score(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=cur
    stagnation=0
    sigma=0.25
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s:
       # subgradient
       conv=normalized_conv(a)
       m=float(np.max(conv))
       if m < best_score -1e-10: best=a.copy(); best_score=m
       # active top indices
       active = np.argsort(conv)[-max(1,min(16, conv.size)):]
       # but if top many small difference, use those above m-tol then top 16
       tol=1e-6*m
       active = np.where(conv >= m - tol)[0]
       if active.size > 16:
          # keep 16 largest
          active = active[np.argsort(conv[active])[-16:]]
       g=np.zeros(a.size)
       for k in active:
          add_coeff_gradient(g,a,int(k))
       if active.size>0:
          g /= active.size
       gp = g - np.mean(g)
       normgp = float(np.linalg.norm(gp))
       scale = float(np.std(a)) + 1e-12
       base_step = 0.1 * scale / (normgp + 1e-12)
       # step candidates dimensionless? If gp normalized by its norm, update = step*gp/norm? Let's use absolute step.
       improved=False
       for step in [base_step, base_step/2, base_step/4, base_step*2, base_step*0.1]:
          b = a - step*gp
          b = np.clip(b,0,None)
          if b.sum() <= 0: continue
          b = normalize_sequence(b)
          sc=objective_score(b)
          if sc < best_score -1e-10: best=b.copy(); best_score=sc
          if sc < cur -1e-10: a=b; cur=sc; improved=True; break
       if improved:
          stagnation=0; sigma=max(0.02, sigma*0.9)
       else:
          stagnation +=1
          # random perturbations
          ntrials = 10 if a.size<8192 else 20? but time.
          for _ in range(12):
             if time.monotonic()-start > budget_s: break
             b=random_perturb(a,rng,sigma)
             sc=objective_score(b)
             if sc < best_score -1e-10: best=b.copy(); best_score=sc
             if sc < cur -1e-10: a=b; cur=sc; improved=True
          if improved:
             stagnation=0
             sigma=max(0.02, sigma*0.95)
          else:
             # shrink/increase sigma periodically
             if stagnation % 20 == 0:
                sigma = min(0.5, sigma*1.05)
                # reset to best occasionally
                a = best.copy()
                cur = objective_score(a)
             if stagnation > 2000: break
    return best,best_score
```
This may be slow because each random trial calls objective_score (FFT) ~0.01s; 20 per loop, okay. 1200s can do many.

But subgradient `active = np.argsort(conv)[-max(...)]` sorts conv length 32767 each loop, O(n log n) ~0.005s. Fine. Could use `np.argpartition` for top. Use:

```
   top = np.argpartition(conv, -16)[-16:]
   active = np.where(conv >= max(conv[top].min() - tol, m - tol))[0]
```

Let's define `q_to_sequence(q,K, powers=None)`:
```
def q_to_sequence(q,K,powers=None):
    if powers is None:
       x=np.linspace(0,1,K); powers=[x**i for i in range(1,len(q)+1)]
    P=np.zeros(K,float)
    for i,pw in enumerate(powers): P += float(q[i])*pw
    P -= P.min()
    # if max > 700, rescale? 
    if P.max() > 700:
       P = P * (700.0 / P.max())
    f=np.exp(P)
    return normalize_sequence(f)
```

`downsample_average(a, m)`:
```
def downsample_average(a,m):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
    if n<=m: return upsample_linear(a,m)
    idx=np.linspace(0,n,m+1).astype(int)
    out=[]
    for i in range(m):
       b=a[idx[i]:idx[i+1]]
       out.append(float(np.mean(b)) if b.size else 0.0)
    return np.array(out)
```

Need be careful with `minimize` args and `basis_eval_q`:
```
def basis_eval_q(q, K, powers):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size != len(powers): return INF
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(q)): return INF
    P = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(len(powers)):
        P += q[i] * powers[i]
    P -= P.min()
    if P.max() > 600.0:
         # reduce dynamic range rather than fail? If we return INF, Nelder-Mead may bounce. Rescale:
         P = P * (500.0 / P.max())
    f = np.exp(P)
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0: return INF
    f = f * (math.sqrt(2.0*K)/s)
    conv = fast_conv_full(f)
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not math.isfinite(m): return INF
    return m
```
Rescale P if max >600 changes shape but keeps finite. Could distort; but huge dynamic equals nearly step, maybe not optimal. Use rescale to 500.

`optimize_basis`:
Need use `minimize` with `args=(K,powers)`, method. It will call `basis_eval_q`. For q arrays, ensure p0? q length deg.
```
def optimize_basis(K, budget_s, rng, deg=6, num_random=8):
    start=time.monotonic()
    x=np.linspace(0,1,K)
    powers=[x**i for i in range(1,deg+1)]
    starts=[]
    for b in [1.2,1.5,2.0,0.8,-1.2]:
       q=np.zeros(deg); q[0]=b; starts.append(q)
    for _ in range(num_random):
       q=np.zeros(deg)
       if deg>0: q[0]=rng.uniform(-2,2)
       if deg>1: q[1:]=rng.uniform(-1.5,1.5,deg-1)
       starts.append(q)
    best_q=None; best_score=INF
    for q0 in starts:
       if time.monotonic()-start >= budget_s-1: break
       sc=basis_eval_q(q0,K,powers)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_q=q0.copy()
       rem=budget_s - (time.monotonic()-start)
       if HAVE_SCIPY and rem > 3:
           try:
              res=minimize(basis_eval_q, q0, args=(K,powers), method='Nelder-Mead',
                           options={'maxiter':300, 'xatol':1e-5, 'fatol':1e-7, 'adaptive':True})
              if res.x is not None:
                 sc2=basis_eval_q(res.x,K,powers)
                 if sc2<best_score:
                    best_score=sc2; best_q=np.asarray(res.x,float).copy()
           except Exception:
              pass
       # If best exists and time, jitter
    if best_q is not None:
       for _ in range(500):
          if time.monotonic()-start >= budget_s-0.2: break
          q=best_q + rng.normal(0,0.05,deg)
          q[0]=q[0] # no
          sc=basis_eval_q(q,K,powers)
          if sc<best_score:
             best_score=sc; best_q=q.copy()
    if best_q is not None:
       seq=q_to_sequence(best_q,K,powers)
    else: seq=None
    return seq,best_score
```
For deg=6 (6 nonlinear terms x..x^6). Random q length 6.

`lp_direction` building matrix could be expensive; we can cache A? f changes each iteration, no.

In `lp_direction`, if n>1500 return None to avoid memory. If `not HAVE_SCIPY` return None.

```
def lp_direction(f,M,lp_time):
   n=f.size
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or n <=0 or n > 1500: return None
   try:
      A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n),float)
      for i,val in enumerate(f):
          if val != 0.0:
             A[i:i+n,i]=val
      c=-np.ones(n,float)
      b=np.full(2*n-1, M, float)
      res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': float(lp_time), 'presolve': True})
      x=None
      if res.success:
         x=res.x
      elif hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
         x=res.x
      if x is None: return None
      x=np.asarray(x,float)
      x=np.clip(x,0,None)
      if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)) or x.sum() <=0: return None
      # check feasibility lightly
      # For large, A@x O(n^2) but n<=1500 okay (2M)
      try:
         resv=A @ x
         if float(np.max(resv)) > M * (1+1e-6) + 1e-8:
             # still return? Could return if slight violation
             if float(np.max(resv)) > M * (1+1e-4) + 1e-6:
                 return None
      except Exception:
         pass
      return x
   except Exception:
      return None
```
If `res.success` false due to time, x may be best feasible? Highs may not. Checking helps.

SOTA directions: If g length n. We'll try raw, mix, lowpass. To avoid too many objective calls, directions maybe 2.

```
def sota_search(f,budget_s,rng,verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic()
    f=np.clip(np.asarray(f,float),0,None)
    if f.size==0: return f, INF
    f=normalize_sequence(f)
    cur=objective_score(f)
    best=f.copy(); best_score=cur
    no_improve=0
    max_no=25
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s and no_improve < max_no:
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       rem=budget_s-elapsed
       if rem < 1: break
       conv=normalized_conv(f)
       m=float(np.max(conv))
       if m < best_score - 1e-10:
          best=f.copy(); best_score=m
       M = m * (1.0 + 1e-7) + 1e-9
       lp_time = min(10.0, max(0.2, rem/50.0))
       g=lp_direction(f,M,lp_time)
       if g is None:
          # if no LP, break to local
          break
       g=normalize_sequence(g)
       directions=[g]
       mix=0.5*g+0.5*f
       directions.append(normalize_sequence(mix))
       if f.size <= 1024:
          lp=lowpass_sequence(g,0.12)
          if lp is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(lp)) and lp.sum()>0:
             directions.append(normalize_sequence(lp))
       improved=False
       t_list=(0.5,0.25,0.1,0.75,1.0,0.05,0.2,0.333,0.02,0.01)
       for d in directions:
          if improved: break
          if not np.all(np.isfinite(d)): continue
          for t in t_list:
             h=(1.0-t)*f + t*d
             h=np.clip(h,0,None)
             if h.sum() <=0: continue
             sc=objective_score(h)
             if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                best=h.copy(); best_score=sc
             if sc < cur - 1e-10:
                f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
       if improved:
          no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          # try uniform t grid
          for d in directions:
             if not np.all(np.isfinite(d)): continue
             for t in np.linspace(0.0,1.0,11):
                h=(1-t)*f+t*d
                h=np.clip(h,0,None)
                if h.sum() <=0: continue
                sc=objective_score(h)
                if sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                    best=h.copy(); best_score=sc
                if sc < cur - 1e-10:
                    f=h; cur=sc; improved=True; break
             if improved: break
          if not improved:
             f=best.copy()
             cur=objective_score(f)
             if no_improve >= max_no: break
    return best,best_score
```
Potential issue: If no LP but we have time, SOTA breaks; local polish can still.

Now, `lowpass_sequence` function. Use FFT or direct:
```
def lowpass_sequence(a, frac=0.12):
   n=a.size
   win=int(max(1, round(n*frac)))
   if win <=1: return a.copy()
   if win >= n: return np.full(n, a.mean())
   k=np.ones(win)/win
   if win <= 128:
       out=np.convolve(a,k,mode='same')
   else:
       L=1
       target=max(2*n, win)
       while L<target: L<<=1
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L); fk=np.fft.rfft(k,L)
       out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fk,L)[:n]
   return np.clip(out,0,None)
```
`np.convolve` with mode same for even win returns length n? Yes.

Now, `add_coeff_gradient` using slice for speed. Let's implement slice correctly:
For k 0..2n-2:
```
def add_coeff_gradient(g,a,k):
    n=a.size
    lo=k
    if lo < 0: lo = 0
    hi=k+1
    if hi > n: hi = n
    # valid j in [lo, hi)
    # indices k-j from k-lo down to k-(hi-1)
    if lo >= hi: return
    g[lo:hi] += 2.0 * a[k-lo : k-hi+1 : -1]? Need stop inclusive.
```
Let's derive: j from lo to hi-1. Index = k-j. For j=lo -> k-lo (max start). For j=hi-1 -> k-hi+1 (min stop inclusive). We want slice from start=k-lo down to stop=k-hi+1 inclusive. Python negative step slice `a[start:stop:-1]` includes start and stops before stop. To include stop, use `stop-1`. So slice `a[k-lo : k-hi+1 - 1 : -1]` = `a[k-lo : k-hi : -1]`. If k-hi == -1? hi can be 0? If hi=0 no. Example k=0,n=3: lo=0,hi=1, start=0, stop=k-hi=-1, slice a[0:-1:-1] empty! Because stop -1 exclusive? Let's test mentally: Python `a[0:-1:-1]`: start 0, stop -1 (last), step -1. Since start == stop? Actually stop -1 represents index 2; moving negative from 0, 0 is before 2? Slice empty. To include index 0, stop should be -1? Wait for slice [1: -1 : -1] on length3: indices 1,0? Actually `a[1:-1:-1]` gives [a1]? Let's recall: stop -1 means index 2, so from 1 down to >2 none? Hmm. Better use `a[k-lo:k-hi:-1]` when k-hi >=0? If min index =0, need stop=-1 to include index 0? For `a[5:-1:-1]` gives indices 5,4,3,2,1,0 (yes, because stop -1 is before 0 in negative direction? Let's confirm: Python slices with negative step: stop is exclusive, but -1 means last element; to include last element, you need stop=-1? Example `a[5:-1:-1]` for len6 includes index 5 to 0? I think yes because it stops before index -1 (which is last), so includes 0. `a[0:-1:-1]` start 0, stop last, no elements because moving negative from 0 cannot go to last. So if start=0 and stop=-1, empty because start is already before stop in negative direction? For `a[5:-1:-1]`, start=5, stop=-1(5?) Wait negative indices: stop -1 refers to index 5 if len6? Actually in slice, -1 is translated to len-1=5. If start=5, stop=5, empty? But I thought `a[5:-1:-1]` returns [a5]? Let's not rely. Python: `list('abcdef'); a[5:-1:-1]` -> ['f']? Start 5, stop 4? For negative step, stop -1 is translated to 5? Hmm. Example `a[5:-2:-1]` -> ['f','e']? stop -2 ->4. The last included is before stop. To include index0, stop should be -1? If -1 translates to -1? For negative step, if stop omitted, goes to beginning. If stop=-1, maybe stop is -1 (not translated?) It accepts negative stop, maybe translates? I'm fuzzy. Safer use `np.arange` for gradient.
```
idx = np.arange(lo, hi)
g[lo:hi] += 2.0 * a[k-idx]
```

`polish_sequence` more refined:
```
def polish_sequence(a,budget_s,rng,verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,None)
    if a.size==0: return a, INF
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    cur=objective_score(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=cur
    no_improve=0
    sigma=0.3
    max_no=3000
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s and no_improve < max_no:
       rem=budget_s - (time.monotonic()-start)
       if rem < 0.5: break
       # subgradient
       conv=normalized_conv(a)
       m=float(np.max(conv))
       if m < best_score -1e-10: best=a.copy(); best_score=m
       active = np.argsort(conv)[-16:]
       # include near max
       near = np.where(conv >= m - 1e-4*m)[0]
       if near.size > 32:
          near = near[np.argsort(conv[near])[-32:]]
       g=np.zeros(a.size)
       cnt=0
       for k in near:
          add_coeff_gradient(g,a,int(k)); cnt+=1
       if cnt>0: g/=cnt
       gp=g - np.mean(g)
       scale=float(np.std(a))
       norm=float(np.linalg.norm(gp))
       if norm > 1e-12 and scale >0:
           base=scale/norm
           for step in (base*0.5, base*0.2, base*0.05, base*0.01, base*2.0):
              b=a - step*gp
              b=np.clip(b,0,None)
              if b.sum() <=0: continue
              b=normalize_sequence(b)
              sc=objective_score(b)
              if sc < best_score -1e-10: best=b.copy(); best_score=sc
              if sc < cur -1e-10: a=b; cur=sc; no_improve=0; break
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           continue? Need random
       improved_sub = (no_improve==0)
       # random perturbations maybe always do few if time
       trials = 8 if a.size < 4096 else 12
       for _ in range(trials):
           if time.monotonic()-start >= budget_s: break
           b=random_perturb(a,rng,sigma)
           sc=objective_score(b)
           if sc < best_score -1e-10:
              best=b.copy(); best_score=sc
           if sc < cur -1e-10:
              a=b; cur=sc; no_improve=0
       # adjust sigma
       if a is best? Hard.
       if no_improve > 50:
          sigma = min(0.6, sigma*1.2)
          if no_improve > 100 and no_improve % 100 ==0:
             a=best.copy(); cur=objective_score(a)
       elif no_improve < 20:
          sigma=max(0.02, sigma*0.9)
       # if no improvement in this whole loop
       if cur == best_score and no_improve_old? We need track.
```

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    b=a.copy()
    # choose mode
    r=rng.random()
    if r < 0.5:
       width=max(1,min(n,int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//8)))))
       start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
       b[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width))
    elif r < 0.8:
       b *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma*0.2, n))
       # linear tilt
       x=np.linspace(-1,1,n)
       tilt=float(rng.normal(0.0, sigma*0.3))
       b *= np.exp(tilt*x)
    else:
       # move mass between two random positions
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if i != j and b[i] > 0:
           amount = b[i] * float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.5))
           b[i] -= amount
           b[j] += amount
    b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    if b.sum() <=0: return a.copy()
    return normalize_sequence(b)
```
Need avoid `np.exp(tilt*x)` under/overflow; sigma small, fine.

Mass move linear helps.

Now, final exact candidates. Need ensure exact score uses list of Python floats. `seq.tolist()`.

`select_best_exact(candidates)`:
```
def select_best_exact(candidates):
    best_seq=None; best_sc=INF
    for seq in candidates:
       if seq is None: continue
       arr=np.asarray(seq,float)
       if arr.size==0: continue
       # exact only if size <= maybe 20000 to avoid too slow; if larger, evaluate via FFT? But we choose small.
       try:
          sc=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
       except NameError:
          sc=objective_score(arr)
       except Exception:
          sc=INF
       sc=float(sc)
       if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best_seq=arr
    return best_seq,best_sc
```

Let's write `basis_eval_q`:
```
def basis_eval_q(q, K, powers):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size != len(powers): return INF
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(q)): return INF
    P = np.zeros(int(K), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(len(powers)):
        if q[i] != 0.0:
            P += float(q[i]) * powers[i]
    P -= P.min()
    if P.max() > 600.0:
        P = P * (500.0 / P.max())
    f = np.exp(P)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300: return INF
    f = f * (math.sqrt(2.0 * int(K)) / s)
    conv = fast_conv_full(f)
    if conv.size == 0: return INF
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not math.isfinite(m): return INF
    return m
```
If P is all NaN? P.min NaN; `not finite q` not enough if overflow in P? q finite and powers finite, P finite. Good.

Let's write `safe_to_list`:
```
def safe_to_list(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return []
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01:
        a = np.ones_like(a)
    return [float(v) for v in a.tolist()]
```
If sum <0.01, set ones. But if n large, sum>0.01.

One more mathematical check: The score under normalization to sqrt(2n) equals max conv. In `objective_score`, after normalize, we compute conv and return max. This is exactly evaluate_sequence score if exact, because evaluate score = 2n*M / S^2 = M when S^2 = 2n. Good.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step function minimizing
  2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

Strategy:
- Work with sequences normalized so sum(a)^2 = 2n; then the objective is
  simply max(conv(a, a)).
- Use fast FFT convolution for search.
- Initial/good shape search with a 6-coefficient positive polynomial-exponential
  family, optimized by Nelder-Mead / Powell.
- Apply the earlier linear-program direction method:
    for current f with M = max(f*f), maximize sum(g) subject to f*g <= M, g >= 0,
    then line-search small convex combinations of f with g / smoothed g.
- Upsample the best curves to a larger length, then polish by subgradient/random
  local search.
- The final returned sequence is selected by exact evaluate_sequence() calls.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

INF = float("inf")


def fast_conv_full(a):
    """Full 1D convolution, using direct convolution for small n and FFT for large n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 128:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    target = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < target:
        L <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:target]
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def normalize_sequence(a, target=None):
    """Normalize non-negative sequence to the given target sum (default sqrt(2n))."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 1e-300 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return np.zeros_like(arr)
    if target is None:
        target = math.sqrt(2.0 * arr.size)
    return arr * (target / s)


def objective_score(a):
    """Fast search objective: exact score under normalization sum^2 = 2n."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return INF
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return INF
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s < 0.01:
        return INF
    arr = arr * (math.sqrt(2.0 * arr.size) / s)
    conv = fast_conv_full(arr)
    if conv.size == 0:
        return INF
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return INF
    return m


def normalized_conv(a):
    """Convolution of sequence after normalization to sum^2 = 2n."""
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return np.zeros(2 * len(a) - 1)
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64) * (math.sqrt(2.0 * len(a)) / s)
    return fast_conv_full(arr)


def exact_score(seq):
    """Call the provided exact evaluator if available."""
    try:
        sc = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except NameError:
        return objective_score(np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64))
    except Exception:
        return INF
    try:
        sc = float(sc)
    except Exception:
        return INF
    if not math.isfinite(sc):
        return INF
    return sc


def upsample_linear(a, n):
    """Linearly interpolate a sequence to length n and renormalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    b = np.interp(xnew, xold, a)
    return normalize_sequence(b)


def upsample_repeat(a, n):
    """Repeat/tile a sequence to length n and renormalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    reps = max(1, int(np.ceil(n / m)))
    b = np.tile(a, reps)[:n]
    return normalize_sequence(b)


def downsample_average(a, m):
    """Average-pool a sequence down to length m."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= m:
        return upsample_linear(a, m)
    idx = np.linspace(0, n, m + 1).astype(int)
    out = np.empty(m, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(m):
        block = a[idx[i]:idx[i + 1]]
        if block.size == 0:
            out[i] = 0.0
        else:
            out[i] = float(np.mean(block))
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def make_exp(n, beta, reverse=False):
    """Exponential ramp sequence."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if reverse:
        x = 1.0 - x
    f = np.exp(beta * x)
    return normalize_sequence(f)


def basis_eval_q(q, K, powers):
    """Objective for f(x)=exp(sum_i q_i x^i) sampled at K points."""
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    if q.size != len(powers):
        return INF
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
        return INF
    P = np.zeros(int(K), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(len(powers)):
        if q[i] != 0.0:
            P += float(q[i]) * powers[i]
    P -= float(np.min(P))
    if P.max() > 600.0:
        P = P * (500.0 / float(P.max()))
    f = np.exp(P)
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return INF
    f = f * (math.sqrt(2.0 * float(K)) / s)
    conv = fast_conv_full(f)
    if conv.size == 0:
        return INF
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return INF
    return m


def q_to_sequence(q, K, powers=None):
    """Convert polynomial-exponential coefficients to normalized sequence."""
    if powers is None:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(K))
        powers = [x ** i for i in range(1, int(len(np.asarray(q))) + 1)]
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    P = np.zeros(int(K), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(min(len(q), len(powers))):
        P += float(q[i]) * powers[i]
    P -= float(np.min(P))
    if P.max() > 600.0:
        P = P * (500.0 / float(P.max()))
    f = np.exp(P)
    return normalize_sequence(f)


def optimize_basis(K, budget_s, rng, deg=6, num_random=10, maxiter_nm=250):
    """Optimize a positive polynomial-exponential family."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    if K <= 0:
        return None, INF
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(K))
    powers = [x ** i for i in range(1, int(deg) + 1)]
    d = len(powers)

    starts = []
    for b in (1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, -1.25):
        q = np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64)
        if d > 0:
            q[0] = float(b)
        starts.append(q)

    for _ in range(int(num_random)):
        q = np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64)
        if d > 0:
            q[0] = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        if d > 1:
            q[1:] = rng.uniform(-1.5, 1.5, d - 1)
        starts.append(q)

    best_q = None
    best_score = INF

    for q0 in starts:
        if time.monotonic() - start >= max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0):
            break
        sc = basis_eval_q(q0, K, powers)
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_q = q0.copy()

        rem = float(budget_s) - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if HAVE_SCIPY and rem > 4.0:
            nm_opts = {
                "maxiter": int(maxiter_nm),
                "xatol": 1e-5,
                "fatol": 1e-7,
                "adaptive": True,
            }
            try:
                res = minimize(
                    basis_eval_q,
                    q0,
                    args=(K, powers),
                    method="Nelder-Mead",
                    options=nm_opts,
                )
                if getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
                    sc2 = basis_eval_q(res.x, K, powers)
                    if sc2 < best_score:
                        best_score = sc2
                        best_q = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            except Exception:
                pass

        rem = float(budget_s) - (time.monotonic() - start)
        if HAVE_SCIPY and rem > 8.0 and best_q is not None:
            pw_opts = {
                "maxiter": 60 if K > 4096 else 100,
                "xtol": 1e-5,
                "ftol": 1e-7,
            }
            try:
                res2 = minimize(
                    basis_eval_q,
                    best_q,
                    args=(K, powers),
                    method="Powell",
                    options=pw_opts,
                )
                if getattr(res2, "x", None) is not None:
                    sc3 = basis_eval_q(res2.x, K, powers)
                    if sc3 < best_score:
                        best_score = sc3
                        best_q = np.asarray(res2.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            except Exception:
                pass

    if best_q is not None:
        for _ in range(400):
            if time.monotonic() - start >= float(budget_s) - 0.1:
                break
            q = best_q + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, d)
            sc = basis_eval_q(q, K, powers)
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_q = q.copy()

    if best_q is None:
        return None, INF
    return q_to_sequence(best_q, K, powers), best_score


def lowpass_sequence(a, frac=0.12):
    """Simple positive low-pass smoothing."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n <= 2:
        return a.copy()
    win = int(max(1, round(n * float(frac))))
    if win <= 1:
        return a.copy()
    if win % 2 == 0:
        win += 1
    if win >= n:
        return np.full(n, float(np.mean(a)), dtype=np.float64)
    k = np.ones(win, dtype=np.float64) / float(win)
    if win <= 160:
        out = np.convolve(a, k, mode="same")
    else:
        L = 1
        target = max(2 * n, n + win)
        while L < target:
            L <<= 1
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        fk = np.fft.rfft(k, L)
        out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fk, L)[:n]
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, None)
    if out.sum() <= 0:
        return a.copy()
    return normalize_sequence(out)


def build_conv_matrix(f):
    """A such that (A @ g)[k] = (f * g)[k]. Dense for modest n."""
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = f.size
    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i, val in enumerate(f):
        if val != 0.0:
            A[i:i + n, i] = val
    return A


def lp_direction(f, M, lp_time):
    """Solve the LP direction problem: max sum(g), f*g <= M, g >= 0."""
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = f.size
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n <= 0 or n > 1500:
        return None
    if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return None
    try:
        A = build_conv_matrix(f)
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=(0, None),
            method="highs",
            options={"time_limit": float(lp_time), "presolve": True},
        )
        x = None
        if getattr(res, "success", False):
            x = res.x
        elif getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
            x = res.x
        if x is None:
            return None
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        x = np.clip(x, 0.0, None)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)) or float(np.sum(x)) <= 0.0:
            return None
        # Light feasibility check.
        try:
            ag = A @ x
            bad = float(np.max(ag))
            if bad > M * (1.0 + 1e-6) + 1e-8:
                if bad > M * (1.0 + 1e-4) + 1e-6:
                    return None
        except Exception:
            pass
        return x
    except Exception:
        return None


def sota_search(f, budget_s, rng, max_no_improve=25):
    """Apply the LP direction iteration, with line search and smoothing fallbacks."""
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size == 0:
        return f, INF
    f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
    if float(np.sum(f)) <= 0.0:
        f = make_exp(f.size, 1.25)
    f = normalize_sequence(f)

    cur = objective_score(f)
    if not math.isfinite(cur):
        f = make_exp(f.size, 1.25)
        cur = objective_score(f)

    best = f.copy()
    best_score = cur
    start = time.monotonic()
    no_improve = 0
    tol = 1e-10

    while time.monotonic() - start < float(budget_s) and no_improve < int(max_no_improve):
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        rem = float(budget_s) - elapsed
        if rem < 1.0:
            break

        conv = normalized_conv(f)
        m = float(np.max(conv))
        if m < best_score - tol:
            best = f.copy()
            best_score = m

        M = m * (1.0 + 1e-7) + 1e-9
        lp_time = min(10.0, max(0.2, rem / 50.0))
        g = lp_direction(f, M, lp_time)

        if g is None:
            break

        g = normalize_sequence(g)

        directions = [g]
        mix = 0.5 * g + 0.5 * f
        directions.append(normalize_sequence(mix))
        if f.size <= 1024:
            lp = lowpass_sequence(g, 0.12)
            if lp is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(lp)) and float(np.sum(lp)) > 0.0:
                directions.append(normalize_sequence(lp))

        improved = False
        t_list = (0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.75, 1.0, 0.05, 0.2, 0.333, 0.02, 0.01)
        old_best = best_score
        old_cur = cur

        for d in directions:
            if improved:
                break
            if d is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(d)):
                continue
            for t in t_list:
                h = (1.0 - t) * f + t * d
                h = np.clip(h, 0.0, None)
                if float(np.sum(h)) <= 0.0:
                    continue
                sc = objective_score(h)
                if sc < best_score - tol:
                    best = h.copy()
                    best_score = sc
                if sc < cur - tol:
                    f = h
                    cur = sc
                    improved = True
                    break

        if not improved:
            # Try smaller/steadier reductions of M in the LP.
            for red in (0.001, 0.003, 0.006):
                M2 = max(m * (1.0 - red), m * 0.9)
                g2 = lp_direction(f, M2, min(8.0, max(0.2, rem / 50.0)))
                if g2 is None:
                    continue
                g2 = normalize_sequence(g2)
                d = g2
                for t in (0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 1.0, 0.05):
                    h = (1.0 - t) * f + t * d
                    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, None)
                    if float(np.sum(h)) <= 0.0:
                        continue
                    sc = objective_score(h)
                    if sc < best_score - tol:
                        best = h.copy()
                        best_score = sc
                    if sc < cur - tol:
                        f = h
                        cur = sc
                        improved = True
                        break
                if improved:
                    break

        if not improved and best_score > old_best - tol and cur > old_cur - tol:
            no_improve += 1
            # Reset to best to avoid drifting while no local improvement is found.
            if no_improve % 4 == 0:
                f = best.copy()
                cur = objective_score(f)
        else:
            no_improve = 0

    return best, best_score


def add_coeff_gradient(g, a, k):
    """Add gradient of convolution coefficient k to g."""
    n = a.size
    lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
    hi = min(n, k + 1)
    if lo >= hi:
        return
    idx = np.arange(lo, hi)
    g[lo:hi] += 2.0 * a[k - idx]


def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    """Random non-negative perturbation used for local polish."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    b = a.copy()
    r = float(rng.random())

    if r < 0.55:
        min_w = max(1, n // 128)
        max_w = max(min_w + 1, n // 8)
        width = int(rng.integers(min_w, max_w))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width))
        b[start:start + width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), width))
    elif r < 0.80:
        b *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma) * 0.25, n))
        tilt = float(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma) * 0.35))
        x = np.linspace(-1.0, 1.0, n)
        b *= np.exp(tilt * x)
    else:
        i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        if i != j and b[i] > 0.0:
            amount = b[i] * float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.6))
            b[i] -= amount
            b[j] += amount

    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)
    if float(np.sum(b)) <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()
    return normalize_sequence(b)


def polish_sequence(a, budget_s, rng):
    """Local polish: subgradient toward active convolution peaks + random perturbations."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a, INF
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.0:
        a = make_exp(a.size, 1.25)
    a = normalize_sequence(a)

    cur = objective_score(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = cur
    tol = 1e-10
    start = time.monotonic()
    sigma = 0.30
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() - start < float(budget_s) and no_improve < 4000:
        if time.monotonic() - start >= float(budget_s):
            break

        old_best = best_score
        old_cur = cur
        improved = False

        # Subgradient step.
        conv = normalized_conv(a)
        m = float(np.max(conv))
        if m < best_score - tol:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = m

        if m > 0.0:
            active = np.argsort(conv)[-16:]
            near = np.where(conv >= m - 1e-4 * m)[0]
            if near.size == 0:
                near = active
            elif near.size > 32:
                near = near[np.argsort(conv[near])[-32:]]

            g = np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
            cnt = 0
            for k in near:
                add_coeff_gradient(g, a, int(k))
                cnt += 1
            if cnt > 0:
                g /= float(cnt)
            gp = g - float(np.mean(g))
            scale = float(np.std(a))
            norm = float(np.linalg.norm(gp))
            if scale > 1e-14 and norm > 1e-14:
                base = scale / norm
                for step in (base * 0.5, base * 0.2, base * 0.05, base * 0.01, base * 2.0):
                    b = a - step * gp
                    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, None)
                    if float(np.sum(b)) <= 0.0:
                        continue
                    b = normalize_sequence(b)
                    sc = objective_score(b)
                    if sc < best_score - tol:
                        best = b.copy()
                        best_score = sc
                    if sc < cur - tol:
                        a = b
                        cur = sc
                        improved = True
                        break

        # Random local search.
        if not improved:
            trials = 6 if a.size > 8192 else 10
            for _ in range(trials):
                if time.monotonic() - start >= float(budget_s):
                    break
                b = random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)
                sc = objective_score(b)
                if sc < best_score - tol:
                    best = b.copy()
                    best_score = sc
                if sc < cur - tol:
                    a = b
                    cur = sc
                    improved = True
                    break

        if improved or best_score < old_best - tol or cur < old_cur - tol:
            no_improve = 0
            sigma = max(0.03, sigma * 0.96)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 25 == 0:
                sigma = min(0.70, sigma * 1.15)
                if no_improve % 100 == 0:
                    a = best.copy()
                    cur = objective_score(a)

    return best, best_score


def safe_to_list(a):
    """Make a safe Python list for evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return []
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01:
        a = np.ones_like(a)
    return [float(x) for x in a.tolist()]


def select_best_exact(candidates):
    """Return candidate with lowest exact evaluate_sequence score."""
    best_seq = None
    best_sc = INF
    for c in candidates:
        if c is None:
            continue
        arr = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            continue
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        lst = safe_to_list(arr)
        sc = exact_score(lst)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_seq = arr.copy()
    return best_seq, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Main search entry point. Returns a list of non-negative heights."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(10.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, budget - 10.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_fft = None
    best_fft_score = INF

    # Quick broad initial candidates.
    for n in (128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192):
        for beta in (1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0):
            a = make_exp(n, beta)
            sc = objective_score(a)
            if sc < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc
                best_fft = a.copy()

    # Optional previous construction, downsampled for search.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hs is not None:
        hs_arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if hs_arr.size > 1 and np.all(np.isfinite(hs_arr)):
            hs_arr = np.clip(hs_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if float(np.sum(hs_arr)) > 0.01:
                hsd = downsample_average(hs_arr, 512)
                sc = objective_score(hsd)
                if sc < best_fft_score:
                    best_fft_score = sc
                    best_fft = hsd.copy()

    # Compressed basis optimization.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    seq_base = None
    if remaining > 60.0:
        bbudget = min(180.0, 0.12 * remaining)
        seq_base, sc_base = optimize_basis(
            512, bbudget, rng,
            deg=6,
            num_random=8,
            maxiter_nm=250 if HAVE_SCIPY else 0,
        )
        if seq_base is not None and sc_base < best_fft_score:
            best_fft_score = sc_base
            best_fft = seq_base.copy()

    # Start for SOTA LP iteration.
    if best_fft is None:
        start512 = make_exp(512, 1.25)
    else:
        start512 = downsample_average(best_fft, 512)

    if seq_base is not None:
        b = upsample_linear(seq_base, 512)
        sc = objective_score(b)
        sc_start = objective_score(start512)
        if sc < sc_start:
            start512 = b

    seq_s1 = None
    seq_s2 = None

    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 120.0:
            s1b = min(300.0, 0.20 * remaining)
            seq_s1, sc1 = sota_search(start512, s1b, rng)
            if sc1 < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc1
                best_fft = np.asarray(seq_s1, dtype=np.float64).copy()

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 240.0:
            for_base = best_fft if best_fft is not None else start512
            start1024 = upsample_linear(for_base, 1024)
            if seq_s1 is not None:
                s1_1024 = upsample_linear(seq_s1, 1024)
                sc1024 = objective_score(s1_1024)
                if sc1024 < objective_score(start1024):
                    start1024 = s1_1024
            s2b = min(800.0, 0.35 * remaining)
            seq_s2, sc2 = sota_search(start1024, s2b, rng)
            if sc2 < best_fft_score:
                best_fft_score = sc2
                best_fft = np.asarray(seq_s2, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    # Choose final length.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 700.0:
        final_n = 8192
    elif remaining > 350.0:
        final_n = 4096
    else:
        final_n = 2048

    # Final-length basis optimization.
    seq_fb = None
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 120.0:
        fbudget = min(240.0, 0.20 * remaining)
        seq_fb, sc_fb = optimize_basis(
            final_n, fbudget, rng,
            deg=6,
            num_random=6 if final_n > 4096 else 10,
            maxiter_nm=120 if final_n > 4096 else 250,
        )
        if seq_fb is not None and sc_fb < best_fft_score:
            best_fft_score = sc_fb
            best_fft = np.asarray(seq_fb, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    # Build final candidates and choose best before local polish.
    final_starts = []
    if best_fft is not None:
        final_starts.append(upsample_linear(best_fft, final_n))
    if seq_s2 is not None:
        final_starts.append(upsample_linear(seq_s2, final_n))
    if seq_s1 is not None:
        final_starts.append(upsample_repeat(seq_s1, final_n))
    if seq_base is not None:
        final_starts.append(upsample_linear(seq_base, final_n))
    if seq_fb is not None:
        final_starts.append(np.asarray(seq_fb, dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    final_starts.append(make_exp(final_n, 1.25))

    best_final = None
    best_final_score = INF
    for a in final_starts:
        if a is None:
            continue
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size != final_n:
            a = upsample_linear(a, final_n)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        if float(np.sum(a)) <= 0.0:
            continue
        sc = objective_score(a)
        if sc < best_final_score:
            best_final_score = sc
            best_final = a.copy()

    if best_final is None:
        best_final = make_exp(final_n, 1.25)
        best_final_score = objective_score(best_final)

    pre_polish = best_final.copy()
    seq_pol = None
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0
    if remaining > 30.0:
        seq_pol, sc_pol = polish_sequence(best_final, remaining, rng)
        if sc_pol < best_fft_score:
            best_fft_score = sc_pol
            best_fft = np.asarray(seq_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    # Exact selection among strong candidates.
    exact_candidates = []
    if best_fft is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(best_fft)
    if seq_s1 is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(seq_s1)
    if seq_s2 is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(seq_s2)
    if seq_base is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(seq_base)
    if seq_fb is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(seq_fb)
    if pre_polish is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(pre_polish)
    if seq_pol is not None:
        exact_candidates.append(seq_pol)
    exact_candidates.append(make_exp(min(1024, final_n), 1.25))

    best_seq, best_exact = select_best_exact(exact_candidates)
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = best_fft if best_fft is not None else make_exp(256, 1.25)

    lst = safe_to_list(best_seq)

    # Final safety: if exact evaluation is somehow invalid, fall back to a simple valid curve.
    try:
        final_check = exact_score(lst)
    except Exception:
        final_check = INF
    if not math.isfinite(final_check):
        lst = safe_to_list(make_exp(256, 1.25))

    return lst
```
